## 3 Methodology

As illustrated in Figure 1, OFAG learns a reusable representation geometry from synthetic attributed graphs, enabling zero-shot clusterin<sub>g</sub> on arbitrar<sub>y</sub> real <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs. After pre-training, the frozen model can be directly applied to unseen real-world graphs for zero-shot clustering without graph-specific fine-tuning.

Theorem 1 (Synthetic-to-real generalization). Let $p _ { \mathrm { r e a l } } ( \mathcal { G } )$ be a distribution over attributed graphs satisfying: (i) the conditional feature distribution $p ( X \mid y )$ , given latent cluster labels �, is a finite mixture ofcontinuous distributions with �-dependent parameters; and (ii) the edge distribution $p ( A \mid y , X )$ follows a pairwise probability model whose log-odds may depend arbitrarily on cluster membership and node features. Then there exist a graph encoder Φ and a CAGM prior $p _ { \mathrm { s y n } } ( \mathcal { G } )$ such that $\Phi _ { \# } p _ { \mathrm { { s y n } } } ( G )$ approximates $p _ { \mathrm { r e a l } } ( \boldsymbol { G } )$ with arbitrarily small total variation distance.

Conditions (i) and (ii) are mild: the<sub>y</sub> assert that both attributes <sub>an</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>er-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sms,</sub> without restrictin<sub>g</sub> to an<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>articular distribution famil<sub>y</sub> or connect<sup>i</sup>v<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>attern.

Proposition 1 (Dimension-agnosticism and feature-permutation eqivariance of SwiFRT). Let � be any permutation of feature indices $\{ 1 , . . . , F \}$ , and let $X _ { \sigma }$ denote � with the �-th column equal to the $\sigma ( f )$ -th column of �. SwiFRT satisfies: (i) the number of trainable parameters is independent ofthe inputfeature dimension $F ;$ and (ii) $\operatorname { S w i F R T } _ { \theta } ( X _ { \sigma } , A ) = \operatorname { S w i F R T } _ { \theta } ( X , A ) _ { \sigma } ,$ where the right-hand side denotes reordering the columns of SwiFRT<sub>�</sub> (�, �) by �.

We train OFAG with an objective derived from the von Mises–Fisher (vMF) distribution [1], which is the canonical <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> distributi<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a on</sub> th<sub>e un</sub>it h<sub>ypersp</sub>h<sub>ere.</sub> P<sub>r</sub>i<sub>or wor</sub>k h<sub>as</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng o</sub>ft<sub>en</sub> b<sub>ecomes more s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en per</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d <sub>on</sub> h<sub>ypersp</sub>h<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons, w</sub>h<sub>ere angu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> similarit<sub>y</sub> rather than vector ma<sub>g</sub>nitude determines the <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> structure [6, 55]. This is suitable for our settin<sub>g</sub> because diferent <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>s may</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> i<sub>ncompa</sub>tibl<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure sca</sub>l<sub>es an</sub>d di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ons.</sub>

Prototypes. Given normalized embeddings {�<sub>�</sub>} from SwiFRT f<sub>or</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s</sub> $y _ { i } \in \{ 1 , . . . , K \}$ , <sup>we</sup> <sup>com</sup>p<sup>ute</sup> <sup>an</sup> episode-specific prototype for each cluster � on the fly:

Compactness loss. Under a vMF mixture with shared concentration $\kappa = 1 / \tau ,$ <sub>,</sub> the <sub>p</sub>osterior assi<sub>g</sub>nment <sub>p</sub>robabilit<sub>y</sub> is

$$

and maximizin<sub>g</sub> the likelihood of the s<sub>y</sub>nthetic assi<sub>g</sub>nments <sub>g</sub>ives th<sub>e compac</sub>t<sub>ness</sub> l<sub>oss</sub>

This term encoura<sub>g</sub>es each re<sub>p</sub>resentation to ali<sub>g</sub>n with its cluster p<sup>rotot</sup>yp<sup>e</sup> <sup>while</sup> <sup>movin</sup>g <sup>awa</sup>y <sup>from</sup> <sup>other</sup> p<sup>rotot</sup>yp<sup>es.</sup>

(9), <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ldi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or</sub> <sub>equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub> l<sub>og-</sub>lik<sub>e</sub>lih<sub>oo</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> <sub>v</sub>MF mixt<sub>u</sub>r<sub>e.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll d<sub>e</sub>ri<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n i<sub>s</sub> in A<sub>ppe</sub>ndix D<sub>.</sub> □

Dispersion loss. The compactness loss alone does not prevent <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es from colla<sub>p</sub>sin<sub>g</sub> into a small re<sub>g</sub>ion of the h<sub>yp</sub>ers<sub>p</sub>here. We therefore add a <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>e dis<sub>p</sub>ersion term:

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { d i s p } } = \frac { 1 } { K } \sum _ { k = 1 } ^ { K } \log \frac { 1 } { K - 1 } \sum _ { j \neq k } \exp \bigl ( \hat { \mu } _ { k } ^ { \intercal } \hat { \mu } _ { j } / \tau \bigr ) ,\tag{11}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> minimiz<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n r<sub>e</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> <sub>pa</sub>ir<sub>w</sub>i<sub>se</sub> <sub>cos</sub>in<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>imil<sub>a</sub>riti<sub>es</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>wee</sub>n dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>ypes</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>encourages</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>sprea</sub>d <sub>across</sub> t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>h</sup><sub>yp</sub>ers<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup>ere.

Remark 1 (Eqiangular tight frame optimum). ${ \mathcal { L } } _ { \mathrm { d i s p } }$ is a log-sum-exp of pairwise cosine similarities between prototypes. When $F \geq K { - } 1 _ { : }$ , its minimizer approaches an equiangular tight frame (ETF): the unique configuration in which all pairwise cosine similarities equal $- 1 / ( K - 1 )$ and prototypes are as uniformly spread across $S ^ { F - \widehat { 1 } }$ as possible. This geometry maximizes inter-cluster angular separation and is mostfavorablefor distinguishing unseen nodes between clusters at inference.

Remark 2 (Complementarity of $\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { c o m p } }$ and ${ \mathcal { L } } _ { \mathrm { d i s p } } )$ . The two terms regularize complementary aspects of the mixture geometry: $\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { c o m p } }$ concentrates each node near its prototype while repelling it from all other prototypes; ${ \mathcal { L } } _ { \mathrm { d i s p } }$ spreads the prototypes themselves across the hypersphere. Together they enforce that nodes concentrate within clusters and that clusters are separated from each other.

Training objective. The overall prior-data objective is

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { L } ( \theta ) = \mathbb { E } _ { ( A , X , y ) \sim p ( \mathcal { D } ) } \big [ \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { c o m p } } ( \theta ; A , X , y ) + \lambda \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { d i s p } } ( \theta ; A , X , y ) \big ] , } \end{array}\tag{12}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\lambda > 0$ balances the two terms. In <sub>p</sub>ractice<sub>,</sub> this ex<sub>p</sub>ectation is a<sub>pp</sub>roximated b<sub>y</sub> Monte Carlo: at each trainin<sub>g</sub> ste<sub>p</sub> we sam<sub>p</sub>le a batch of s<sub>y</sub>nthetic <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs<sub>,</sub> encode them with SwiFRT<sub>,</sub> com<sub>pu</sub>te <sub>on-</sub>th<sub>e-</sub>fl<sub>y pro</sub>t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>ypes</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s, an</sub>d b<sub>ac</sub>k<sub>-propaga</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h th<sub>e com</sub>bi<sub>ne</sub>d l<sub>oss.</sub>

## 3.5 Connection to Prior-Data Fitted Networks

T<sup>h</sup>e prior-<sup>fi</sup>tte<sup>d</sup> c<sup>l</sup>ustering o<sup>b</sup>jective is not mere<sup>l</sup>y a convenient trainin<sub>g</sub> reci<sub>p</sub>e<sub>;</sub> it has a <sub>p</sub>recise Ba<sub>y</sub>esian inter<sub>p</sub>retation that <sub>p</sub>laces OFAG within the PFN framework [43, 44].

Proposition 3 (OFAG as a Prior-Data Fitted Network). Minimizing $\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } _ { ( A , X , y ) \sim p ( \mathcal { D } ) } [ \mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { c o m p } } ( \theta ; A , X , y ) ] } \end{array}$ is equivalent to minimizing the Prior-Data Negative Log-Likelihood of a Bayesian model with prior $p ( \mathcal { D } )$ and a vMFmixture likelihood, with empty context $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c } } = \emptyset$ Consequently, a suficiently expressive OFAG trained to convergence approximates the posterior predictive distribution induced by �(D).

The full <sub>p</sub>roof is in A<sub>pp</sub>endix E. □

## 3.6 Zero-Shot Inference

After <sub>p</sub>re-trainin<sub>g,</sub> all <sub>p</sub>arameters are frozen. Given an unseen attributed <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h (�, �), OFAG <sub>p</sub>roduces node embeddin<sub>g</sub>s in a sin<sub>g</sub>le f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass,</sub>

$$
Z = \mathrm { S w i F R T } _ { \theta ^ { * } } ( X , A ) , \qquad z _ { i } = Z _ { i , : } / \| Z _ { i , : } \| _ { 2 } ,\tag{13}
$$

Datasets. Baselines. OFAG is strongest on Reddit, leading all methods b<sub>y</sub> a wide mar<sub>g</sub>in (NMI 85.94 vs. S3GC 83.45; F1 81.17 vs. SAGSC 70.43); the +10.7 F1 <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub> su<sub>gg</sub>ests that the CAGM <sub>p</sub>rior’s diverse cluster-<sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ortion sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> is es<sub>p</sub>eciall<sub>y</sub> beneficial for Reddit’<sub>s</sub> imb<sub>a</sub>l<sub>a</sub>n<sub>ce</sub>d <sub>co</sub>mm<sub>u</sub>nit<sub>y</sub> <sub>s</sub>tr<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>u</sub>r<sub>e.</sub> On C<sub>o</sub>r<sub>a,</sub> Ph<sub>o</sub>t<sub>o,</sub> <sub>a</sub>nd ArXi<sub>v,</sub>

This <sub>g</sub>a<sub>p</sub> widens further when accountin<sub>g</sub> f<sub>or</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>er</sub> t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>mus</sub>t b<sub>e</sub> t<sub>une</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> <sub>o</sub>ft<sub>en</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>ens</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>repea</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d runs, <sub>p</sub>ushin<sub>g p</sub>ractical s<sub>p</sub>eedu<sub>p</sub>s to the thousand-fold level. ## 4.4 Ablation: Model Components

## 5 Conclusion

We <sub>p</sub>resented OFAG, the first one-for-all foundation model for attributed <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h clusterin<sub>g</sub>. B<sub>y</sub> ada<sub>p</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> the Prior-data Fitted Network <sub>p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m to the unsu<sub>p</sub>ervised <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h settin<sub>g</sub>, OFAG is <sub>p</sub>re-trained once on s<sub>y</sub>nthetic <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs sam<sub>p</sub>led from the CAGM <sub>p</sub>rior and a<sub>pp</sub>lied to arbitrar<sub>y</sub> real-world <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs in a sin<sub>g</sub>le forward <sub>p</sub>ass. Ex<sub>p</sub>eriments on ten datasets show that a sin<sub>g</sub>le frozen OFAG model achieves the b<sub>es</sub>t <sub>mean per</sub>f<sub>ormance w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> b<sub>e</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>as</sub>t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d <sub>overa</sub>ll<sub>.</sub>

Connection to $\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { c o m p } } .$ Th<sub>e comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a nega</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> l<sub>og-</sub>lik<sub>e</sub>lih<sub>oo</sub>d <sub>u</sub>nd<sub>e</sub>r th<sub>e v</sub>MF mixt<sub>u</sub>r<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub>:

Remark 3. The cancellation o ${ } ^ { C } Z _ { d } ( \kappa )$ in Eq. (27) is a distinctive property ofthe vMF distribution: its normalization constant does not depend on the mean direction �<sub>�</sub>. This property does not hold for Gaussian distributions with class-specific covariances, where the logdeterminant log $\left| \Sigma _ { k } \right|$ would remain in the objective and introduce additional terms.

## E Proof of Proposition 3

Proof. The Prior-Data Ne<sub>g</sub>ative Lo<sub>g</sub>-Likelihood (Prior-Data NLL) for a PFN is defined as [43, 44]:

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { P F N } } ( \theta ) = \mathbb { E } _ { \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c } } \cup \{ ( x , y ) \} \sim p ( \mathcal { D } ) } \left[ - \log q _ { \theta } ( y \mid x , \mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c } } ) \right] .\tag{29}
$$

Identifying context and query. Since no cluster labels are <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e a</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> ti<sub>me,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> PFN <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> $\mathcal { D } _ { \mathrm { c } } = \emptyset$ . <sup>Th</sup>e <sub>q</sub>uer<sub>y</sub> <sub>se</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>a</sub>k<sub>en</sub> t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e a</sub>ll <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n a syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c grap</sub>h $( A , X , y ) \sim p ( { \mathcal { D } } )$ Each node � corres<sub>p</sub>onds to a <sub>q</sub>uer<sub>y p</sub>oint $x = z _ { i }$ <sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l $y = y _ { i } ,$ <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $z _ { i } = Z _ { i , : } / \Vert Z _ { i , : } \Vert _ { 2 }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> ℓ<sub>2</sub>-n<sub>o</sub>rm<sub>a</sub>liz<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f S<sub>w</sub>iFRT<sub>.</sub>

Identifying the predictive distribution. ## G Baseline Details

We follow P<sub>y</sub>AGC [37] and com<sub>p</sub>are OFAG with re<sub>p</sub>resentative attributed <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h clusterin<sub>g</sub> methods. For clarit<sub>y,</sub> we <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> the baselines into three cate<sub>g</sub>ories.

## G.1 Traditional and Non-Parametric Methods

• KMeans [42] clusters the raw node features directly and ignores <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>. It serves as a basic feature-onl<sub>y</sub> baseline.

• Node2Vec [14] learns structural node embeddings via biased <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> <sub>wa</sub>lk<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>en</sub> <sub>app</sub>li<sub>es</sub> �<sub>-means.</sub> It <sub>serves</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure-</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne.</sub>

• SSGC [67] uses simplified spectral graph convolution to prop-<sub>aga</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>us</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> It i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>non-parame</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> �<sub>-means.</sub>

• SAGSC [12] combines graph smoothing with subspace clustering.
